│                                                                              │

│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[solver]'.                                       │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │

│ executed):\n  - Read image bytes: img_bytes =                                │
│ open("/app/code.png","rb").read()\n  - ho = sha256(img_bytes) as raw 32-byte │
│ digest\n  - mid = first 10 characters of ho.hex() encoded as ASCII bytes     │
│ (b&#x27;f4d0330407&#x27;)\n  - sep = b"::"\n  - salt = the literal UTF-8     │
│ string "6`0000bench-salt&#x27;"\n  - data = ho + mid + sep +                 │

│ Summary of task and solution                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│  • Goal: Implement the logic shown in the image /app/code.png and write the  │
│    final printed value to /app/output.txt. Hint: result starts with          │
│    "bee26a".                                                                 │
│  • Challenge: The pseudocode was only present as image pixels; OCR was noisy │
│    and environment lacked image libraries, so exact salt and concatenation   │
│    had to be determined and verified.                                        │
│  • Final implemented algorithm (exact, as executed):                         │
│     • Read image bytes: img_bytes = open("/app/code.png","rb").read()        │
│     • ho = sha256(img_bytes) as raw 32-byte digest                           │
│     • mid = first 10 characters of ho.hex() encoded as ASCII bytes           │
│       (b'f4d0330407')                                                        │
│     • sep = b"::"                                                            │
│     • salt = the literal UTF-8 string "6`0000bench-salt'"                    │
│     • data = ho + mid + sep + salt.encode('utf-8')                           │
│     • H = sha256(data).hexdigest()                                           │
│     • Write H to /app/output.txt                                             │
│  • Result written to /app/output.txt:                                        │
│    bee26afaa0ad4479402616753a64f00d7266f15b6be08bede4f848c8a9d05d6a          │
│  • Verification: Tester reproduced the exact steps and parameters (ho, mid,  │
│    sep, salt, data lengths) and confirmed the computed hash equals the file  │
│    content.                                                                  │
│  • Key findings/lessons:                                                     │
│     • Visual-only pseudocode requires exact transcription; small             │
│       character/encoding differences (backtick vs apostrophe, separator      │
│       bytes, hex vs raw bytes) change the output.                            │
│     • When OCR is unreliable, providing the exact script or salt string is   │
│       essential for reproducibility.                                         │
│     • The provided implementation and exact salt resolved ambiguity and      │
│       allowed deterministic verification.                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Task status: Completed and verified — the correct hash (above) is in         │
│ /app/output.txt.                                                             │
